- Liljeberg: its separation of violation from remedy is preserved; its civil Rule 60(b)(6) procedure is not transplanted to this criminal direct appeal.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Eleventh Circuit judgment is affirmed, 9–0, under the five-Justice objective-appearance rule. No recusal violation is found, and no retrial, reassignment or remand is ordered.

### Party status and questions presented

#### Izumi Seimitsu Kogyo Kabushiki Kaisha v. U.S. Philips Corp., 510 U.S. 27

**Docket or dockets:** No. 92-1123.

**Decided:** 1993-11-30.

##### Review of a denied intervention application is distinct from review as a party

**Holding:** Under 28 U.S.C. §1254(1), an unsuccessful applicant for intervention may seek review of the denial, but cannot obtain review of the parties' underlying judgment on the strength of an unreviewed intervention denial or a financial interest alone. Because Izumi's denial of intervention remains undisturbed, Izumi lacks the party status necessary to press the settlement-vacatur question in this petition; the Court does not decide whether intervention should have been allowed.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court supported by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg, seven Justices; Blackmun and Stevens dissent. This stated threshold ground supports the dismissal and carries procedural authority, without a settlement-vacatur holding.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- *Automobile Workers v. Scofield*, 382 U.S. 205 (1965): applies its route for an applicant denied intervention to seek review of that denial; no automatic party status or right to review the underlying judgment follows from applying to intervene.

##### The intervention question was not fairly included and no exception is warranted

**Holding:** Rule 14.1(a) creates a strong prudential presumption against deciding a question not stated in the certiorari petition or fairly included in a stated question, subject to exceptional circumstances; a separate intervention denial is not fairly included merely because it must be overturned before the Court can reach a settlement-vacatur question. Neither discussion in the petition's body and later merits briefing nor the antecedent relationship establishes exceptional urgency or economy on this record, so the Court declines review of intervention and dismisses the writ as improvidently granted, leaving vacatur undecided.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court supported by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg, seven Justices; Blackmun and Stevens dissent. The reasoned dismissal establishes this question-presentation ruling, not the merits of the judgment below.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- *Yee v. City of Escondido*, 503 U.S. 519 (1992), question-presented portion: applies the distinction between related independent questions and subsidiary questions, with the narrow prudential exception retained; it does not convert the limit into a jurisdictional prohibition.
- *Automobile Workers v. Scofield*, 382 U.S. 205 (1965): its review route remains available, but does not dispense with presenting the intervention question.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Statutory party status, Article III interest and prudential question presentation are separate. The Court does not decide intervention entitlement, settlement vacatur, collateral estoppel, or a new construction of §2106. Munsingwear, Karcher and Cardinal Chemical receive no controlling settlement-vacatur treatment.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The writ is dismissed as improvidently granted, 7–2. The Federal Circuit's intervention denial and vacatur orders remain undisturbed; the Court neither affirms their merits nor reinstates a district-court judgment or orders a new remand.

#### California v. Roy

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-2025.

**Decided:** November 4, 1996.

For the other questions in California v. Roy, No. 95-2025, decided November 4, 1996, see Criminal Procedure - Constitutional trial error on habeas (This personal-intent omission permits harmless-error review under Chapman; Necessary equivalence is sufficient, but not the exclusive permissible inquiry; The limited remand preserves the record, argument and remedial boundaries).

##### The existing official appellate party may seek review

**Holding:** Section 1254(1) requires a petitioner who was a party in the court of appeals; on this record, that court's repeated identification of the Attorney General as an official-capacity respondent and the timely petition expressly naming the incumbent in that capacity satisfy the requirement. The Court accepts the existing official party's identity despite the contrary earlier custodial pleadings; it neither adds a party nor holds that a caption, counsel's participation or a State's interest invariably establishes party status.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Part I, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices. Stone-Zsela agrees only with the threshold disposition.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Official-title and substitution rules continue an existing party; they do not create one. No custodial petitioner is added, and counsel's participation or the State's interest alone does not establish party status. The separate status of the People is not decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Scofield and Perini retain their actual-party requirement; Smith is applied to identification in a timely filing without dispensing with that requirement.

### Settlement-enforcement jurisdiction

#### Kokkonen v. Guardian Life Insurance Co. of America, 511 U.S. 375

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-263.

**Decided:** 1994-05-16.

##### Postdismissal contract enforcement requires a jurisdictional basis

**Holding:** After a Rule 41(a)(1)(ii) stipulated dismissal that neither incorporates settlement obligations nor retains jurisdiction, a court has no ancillary or inherent jurisdiction to enforce the separate settlement merely because it ended a federal action; an independent jurisdictional basis is required. Neither factual interdependence nor authority to manage proceedings supports jurisdiction when the settlement-breach facts are separate from the former claim and breach violates no obligation in the dismissal order; judicial awareness or approval of the agreement is insufficient.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt the rule, application, and reversal with remand.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Willy v. Coastal Corp., 503 U.S. 131 (1992): distinguished; collateral Rule 11 authority does not create jurisdiction over a new settlement contract.
- Julian v. Central Trust Co., 193 U.S. 93 (1904): distinguished by its express reservation concerning judicially conveyed property; broad language is not general settlement-enforcement authority.
- Moore v. New York Cotton Exchange, 270 U.S. 593 (1926): its factual-interdependence basis is inapplicable to these distinct controversies.
- Chambers v. NASCO, Inc., 501 U.S. 32 (1991): its authority over litigation misconduct does not convert every settlement breach into interference with judicial proceedings.
- McNutt v. General Motors Acceptance Corp., 298 U.S. 178 (1936): applied; the party asserting federal jurisdiction bears the burden of establishing it.

##### A lawful dismissal order may make settlement compliance enforceable

**Holding:** A court may enforce settlement obligations through ancillary jurisdiction when, within its lawful dismissal authority, its order incorporates those obligations or expressly retains jurisdiction over the settlement, making breach a violation of the judicial disposition. Under Rule 41(a)(2) the court may impose such terms in its discretion; under Rule 41(a)(1)(ii) it may incorporate or retain jurisdiction if the parties agree, but private consent alone, awareness of the bargain, or an attempted later addition after jurisdiction has ended does not create jurisdiction.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this qualification defining the boundary of the jurisdictional holding.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Julian's actual reservation-based holding remains effective; Rule 41's distinct mechanisms are applied without creating a mandatory retention rule. Rule 60(b) reopening is reserved and is not treated as contract enforcement.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court decides jurisdiction, not breach, specific performance, damages, Rule 60(b) reopening or diversity for a new claim. Neither private consent nor postdismissal judicial rewriting supplies lost jurisdiction.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Ninth Circuit is reversed and remanded for dismissal of the settlement-enforcement motion for lack of the asserted ancillary jurisdiction. No breach adjudication or reopening follows. State contract enforcement and a new federal action with an independent jurisdictional basis remain distinct.

### Collateral appeals from settlement orders

#### Digital Equipment Corp. v. Desktop Direct, Inc., 511 U.S. 863

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-405.

**Decided:** 1994-06-06.

##### A private settlement does not supply the required collateral-order importance

**Holding:** An order refusing effect to an ordinary private settlement does not qualify for immediate appeal under §1291 merely because the agreement promises freedom from trial. Assuming conclusiveness and separateness, the category lacks the public importance required to excuse final-judgment review; irretrievable litigation burdens and the particular agreement's value, clarity or alleged violation do not enlarge appellate jurisdiction.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this independently sufficient ground for affirmance.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Cohen v. Beneficial Industrial Loan Corp., 337 U.S. 541: applies its narrow category of collateral decisions rather than a general hardship exception.
- Coopers & Lybrand v. Livesay, 437 U.S. 463: applies the cumulative conclusiveness, separateness, importance and effective-unreviewability requirements.
- Lauro Lines s.r.l. v. Chasser, 490 U.S. 495: applies category-based finality to a contractual claim despite practical burdens of litigation; its importance rationale distinguishes private bargains from recognized public immunities.
- Abney v. United States, 431 U.S. 651: distinguishes the constitutional interest against a second criminal trial, leaving its immediate-review rule intact.
- Mitchell v. Forsyth, 472 U.S. 511: distinguishes the public-law immunity from suit without expanding its substantive coverage.
- Puerto Rico Aqueduct & Sewer Authority v. Metcalf & Eddy, January 12, 1993: preserves immediate review of qualifying Eleventh Amendment immunity denials; a private settlement is a different category.

**Independent alternative holding:** Even apart from the dispute over importance terminology, ordinary settlement rights of this category remain adequately vindicable after final judgment and therefore fail the collateral-order effective-unreviewability requirement. Review of protection against ultimate liability, together with lawful contract enforcement and district-court protections when their own requirements are met, is adequate in this setting despite the irretrievable experience and expense of litigation.

**Proposition-level authority for alternative holding:** Part II of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. Eight Justices adopt this independently sufficient alternative; Stone-Zsela does not join Part II.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Neither alternative ground resolves the validity of settlement rescission, actual fraud, breach damages, sanctions, the district court's power to reopen, or trademark liability. Section 1292(b) remains separate: it requires written district certification of a controlling legal question, substantial ground for difference of opinion, possible material advancement of termination, timely application and appellate permission. No satisfaction of those conditions is found and no such appeal is certified here.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Lauro Lines s.r.l. v. Chasser, 490 U.S. 495: applies adequate postjudgment vindication despite loss of an asserted contractual litigation advantage.
- Cohen v. Beneficial Industrial Loan Corp., 337 U.S. 541: applies effective unreviewability as a distinct limitation on immediate review.
- Kokkonen v. Guardian Life Insurance Co. of America, May 16, 1994: preserves the independent jurisdictional requirements for contract enforcement and its reservation of Rule 60(b) reopening; no breach remedy is found available on these facts.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Tenth Circuit's dismissal of the interlocutory appeal is affirmed. No settlement-rescission merits ruling, contract remedy, trademark judgment or §1292(b) certification or permission is entered.

### Federal common law and acquired state claims

#### O’Melveny & Myers v. FDIC, 512 U.S. 79

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-489.

**Decided:** 1994-06-13.

##### These acquired California claims carry California imputation law

**Holding:** On the pre-FIRREA framework, California law governs imputation and related defenses to the institution's California negligence and fiduciary-duty claims acquired by the federal receiver: fund protection, greater recovery and general administrative uniformity do not establish the particular significant conflict with federal policy required for the proposed federal exception. The Ninth Circuit's federal-common-law ruling is reversed and the case remanded to determine the preserved state-law questions, without directing judgment for either party.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt the same pre-FIRREA ground, independently sufficient to reject the proposed rule without deciding FIRREA's temporal reach.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Erie Railroad v. Tompkins, 304 U.S. 64: applies the absence of general federal common law to privately generated state causes; exceptional federal fields are not abolished.
- Wallis v. Pan American Petroleum Corp., 384 U.S. 63: applies its requirement of an identified significant conflict with federal policy; ordinary federal financial interests are insufficient here.
- Boyle v. United Technologies Corp., 487 U.S. 500: preserves federal-interest and conflict prerequisites for exceptional displacement; the necessary conflict is absent in these acquired claims.
- United States v. Kimbell Foods, Inc., 440 U.S. 715: distinguishes federally generated program rights and preserves its rejection of automatic nationwide uniformity even there.
- United States v. Texas, April 5, 1993: distinguishes the established federal delay-compensation obligation on an assumed federal debt; it supplies no new receiver exemption from state tort defenses.
- FDIC v. Meyer, February 23, 1994: preserves the separation of sovereign consent, federal-party status and substantive cause of action; this action remains state created.

##### FIRREA, if applicable, supplies no additional anti-imputation rule

**Holding:** If FIRREA governs these claims, §1821(d)(2)(A)(i) transfers the institution's rights, titles, powers and privileges to the FDIC subject to the legal incidents of those rights and the federal exceptions Congress actually enacted; it does not authorize adding the proposed federal exemption from California imputation law. The Court leaves FIRREA's temporal reach unresolved because the independently sufficient pre-FIRREA analysis produces the same state-law remand.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this conditional statutory construction and its temporal reservation. It is not a finding that FIRREA applies retrospectively.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court does not decide California imputation, adverse-interest exceptions, estoppel, engagement scope, duty, breach, causation, recoverable client losses, assigned investor claims, a statute-of-frauds defense, or FIRREA's temporal reach. Reversal does not reinstate the unexplained district-court summary judgment. The complaint does not seek reimbursement of the investors' rescission payments.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Erie Railroad v. Tompkins, 304 U.S. 64: preserves state substantive law where no enacted or otherwise valid federal displacement is established.
- Boyle v. United Technologies Corp., 487 U.S. 500: remains available within its actual exceptional-federal-law requirements; this construction does not occupy every federal common-law field.
- Landgraf v. USI Film Products, April 26, 1994: preserves its temporal-direction and new-consequences framework; FIRREA's temporal application is unnecessary to decide and is not presumed.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Ninth Circuit is reversed and the case remanded for preserved California-law questions. The unexplained original summary judgment is not reinstated automatically. No liability, damages or refund is ordered; FIRREA's temporal reach remains unresolved.

### Voting Rights Act comparisons and unequal opportunity

#### Johnson v. De Grandy, 512 U.S. 997

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 92-519, 92-593 and 92-767.

**Decided:** 1994-06-30.

**Companion or consolidated matters:** Johnson v. De Grandy, No. 92-519; De Grandy v. Johnson, No. 92-593; United States v. Florida, No. 92-767. Holder v. Hall, decided June 30, 1994, precedes this decision in the expressly coordinated same-day sequence.

For the other questions in Johnson v. De Grandy, 1994-06-30, Nos. 92-519, 92-593 and 92-767, see Election Law — Necessary voting conditions do not require maximization; Election Law — Proportional opportunity is relevant evidence, not a safe harbor; Election Law — The House liability and dependent remedy cannot stand on this record; Election Law — The Senate judgment stands because no violation was proved.

##### The state validation does not preclude the reserved factual claims

**Holding:** Under 28 U.S.C. §1738, Florida's validation receives its actual state-law preclusive scope, including its express preservation of later factual Section 2 challenges; it therefore does not bar these private claims. The availability of a return to state court does not require exhaustion of that optional procedure before this preserved federal litigation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part I-A, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Ginsburg; seven Justices directly adopt this limited rule. Thomas and Scalia do not join the opinion, and their different statutory disposition supplies no additional published joinder.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Marrese v. American Academy of Orthopaedic Surgeons, 470 U.S. 373: applies the state-law-first rule for the preclusive effect of a state judgment; no additional federal preclusion is imposed.
- Allen v. McCurry, 449 U.S. 90: preserves ordinary preclusion and its qualifications; creates no categorical federal-forum exemption for Voting Rights Act claims.
- Growe v. Emison, February 23, 1993: follows its requirement to respect the legally operative state plan and distinguishes its obsolete-plan defect; no enlarged party-preclusion rule follows.

##### The United States brings an original enforcement suit

**Holding:** Rooker and Feldman do not bar the United States' independent Section 2 enforcement suit against this plan: the United States was not a party to the state validation and seeks no lower-federal appellate review of a state judgment entered against it. The holding does not decide every nonparty, privity, intervention or intertwined-claim question or permit review of a preclearance determination.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part I-B, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Ginsburg; seven Justices directly adopt the original-suit distinction on these facts. Thomas and Scalia publish no join in this part.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Rooker v. Fidelity Trust Co., 263 U.S. 413: applies the limit on lower-federal appellate review of state judgments; this independent enforcement action does not invoke that authority.
- District of Columbia Court of Appeals v. Feldman, 460 U.S. 462: preserves the appellate/original distinction without deciding every possible relationship between nonparties and a state judgment.
- Shaw v. Reno, June 28, 1993: preserves the designated channel and nonreviewability limits for federal preclearance; those limits do not immunize a state plan from an independent statutory claim.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The House liability judgment and dependent Modified De Grandy Plan 268 remedy are reversed in No. 92-519; the Senate judgment is affirmed in Nos. 92-593 and 92-767, each 7–2. Implementation must follow lawful election administration. No new liability trial, extra Senate district, Supreme Court replacement map or special election timetable is ordered. Congressional proceedings, the Escambia settlement, dismissed claims and preclearance determinations remain undisturbed.

### Agency aggrievement and statutory appellate review

#### Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs v. Newport News Shipbuilding & Dry Dock Co.

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1783.
**Decided:** March 21, 1995.

##### The Director's general administrative interest does not confer independent review

**Holding:** The Director's general interest in correct administration of the Longshore and Harbor Workers' Compensation Act does not by itself make the Director adversely affected or aggrieved under 33 U.S.C. §921(c) by this employee's unappealed benefits award. The refusal to entertain that challenge is affirmed; no rule of categorical governmental incapacity is adopted, and the separate Special Fund disposition remains undisturbed.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (O'Connor), eight Justices; Ginsburg concurs in the judgment only.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Connecticut National Bank v. Germain, 503 U.S. 249: applies respect for the requirements of the particular statutory route; its bankruptcy appellate holding remains unchanged.
- Brown v. Gardner, December 12, 1994: applies fidelity to enacted conditions rather than administrative additions; its benefits-causation holding is not expanded.
- Director, OWCP v. Perini North River Associates, 459 U.S. 297: distinguishes respondent participation from a Director-initiated appeal; the respondent issue remains reserved.
- Director, OWCP v. Greenwich Collieries, June 20, 1994: preserves its persuasion-burden rules and treats them as distinct from statutory appellate entitlement.
- Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife, June 12, 1992: leaves its Article III holdings unchanged; the statutory disposition makes a new constitutional rule unnecessary.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No categorical governmental incapacity, respondent-status rule, new Article III holding, disability merits or Fund liability is decided. Express statutory review rights and genuine financial or independently statutory injury remain open.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The refusal to entertain the Director's employee-award challenge is affirmed, 9–0. The Fourth Circuit's separate Special Fund reversal and remand remains intact. No disability merits, Fund liability or respondent-status ruling is made.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Ingalls Shipbuilding, Inc. v. Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs, February 18, 1997, No. 95-1081: resolves the reserved respondent question in a live employer-initiated appeal under then-effective Rule 15(a); independent Director review, the Special Fund question and continuation after private adversity ends remain distinct.

#### Ingalls Shipbuilding, Inc. v. Director, Office of Workers' Compensation Programs

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-1081.
**Decided:** February 18, 1997.

For the separate questions in Ingalls Shipbuilding, Inc. v. Director, Office of Workers' Compensation Programs, No. 95-1081 decided February 18, 1997, see Maritime Law — Longshore compensation and third-party settlements (A spouse's contingent death-benefit claim does not trigger predeath forfeiture).

##### The Director may respond in this private-party review

**Holding:** The Director of OWCP, as the Secretary of Labor's designated representative, is a proper respondent under the then-effective Federal Rule of Appellate Procedure 15(a) in an employer-initiated appeal from a Benefits Review Board order while the private parties maintain a live controversy. This respondent role does not authorize an independent Director appeal based only on a general administrative interest or decide whether the Director could alone continue the controversy after all private adversity ends.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II of O'Connor's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; seven Justices. Scalia and Thomas dissent only from this component.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Rule 15(a), rather than representation provisions alone, supplies respondent status. The designation regulation and §921a identify representation, with §921a expressly excepting this Court. No independent Director appeal, relief outside the private controversy, Special Fund case, continuation after all private adversity ends or merits-deference rule is decided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Director remains a respondent in the employer-initiated review; affirmance leaves the other ungranted lower rulings undisturbed.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Newport News's independent-appeal bar based solely on general administrative interest survives. Its reserved respondent issue is resolved for this live private-party appeal only; its Special Fund reservation remains.

### Agency authority to seek Supreme Court review

#### Federal Election Commission v. NRA Political Victory Fund

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1151.
**Decided:** December 6, 1994.

##### The Commission lacks independent certiorari authority for this FECA enforcement action

**Holding:** Section 437d(a)(6)'s authority to litigate and appeal civil enforcement actions through the Commission's general counsel does not displace 28 U.S.C. §518(a)'s allocation of Supreme Court representation in this FECA corporate-contribution action. The Attorney General's particular-case direction exception remains available, and the express certiorari powers in 26 U.S.C. §§9010(d) and 9040(d) remain confined to actions in which the Commission appears under those respective funding sections.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's opinion, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer: six of eight participating Justices. Ginsburg did not participate.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- United States v. Providence Journal Co., 485 U.S. 693 (1988): applies its separation of lower-court litigation authority from Supreme Court representation while retaining ordinary statutory construction of congressional exceptions; no special verbal formula is imposed.
- Connecticut National Bank v. Germain, 503 U.S. 249 (1992): applies its preservation of coexisting statutory routes and their conditions; enforcement authority and Supreme Court representation can operate together.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The holding concerns this affirmative FECA enforcement petition. It does not deny the Commission the express powers supplied for funding litigation, construe every agency's litigation statute, or eliminate section 518(a)'s particular-case direction exception.

##### Authorization after the statutory petition period does not ratify this filing

**Holding:** Even assuming that the Solicitor General's May 26, 1994 authorization fully adopted the Commission's January 18 petition, it cannot relate back to validate this unauthorized invocation after the period in which an authorized petition could be filed had expired. Section 2101(c) allows ninety days and permits a Justice, for good cause, to extend the application period by no more than sixty days; the asserted authorization falls outside even that maximum under either supplied judgment date.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's opinion, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer: six of eight participating Justices. Ginsburg did not participate.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Cook v. Tullis, 85 U.S. (18 Wall.) 332 (1873): applies the requirement that a ratifying principal have capacity both at the original act and at ratification, with intervening rights preserved; relation back does not supply capacity after this statutory petition period.
- Dodge v. United States, 272 U.S. 530 (1926): distinguishes adoption of an unauthorized seizure where the forfeiture prerequisites were satisfied from authorization of appellate review after the statutory filing period.
- Smith v. Barry, 502 U.S. 244 (1992): distinguishes a timely document that functionally supplies required appellate notice from a filing lacking governmental representation authority; the functional-notice holding remains intact.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court decides no universal rule that all representation defects are incurable. It makes no finding that a Justice granted an extension or that the amended lower opinion restarted time. The Solicitor General's authorization is distinct from any Commission resolution addressing its composition; neither constitutional validity nor structural ratification is reached.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The writ is dismissed for want of jurisdiction. The D.C. Circuit judgment remains in place without Supreme Court endorsement of its constitutional merits. No ruling is made on congressional membership, structural severability or ratification, contribution liability or renewed enforcement.

### Collateral appeals from municipal liability defenses

#### Swint v. Chambers County Commission, 514 U.S. 35

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1636.
**Decided:** March 1, 1995.

##### Ordinary municipal liability remains reviewable after final judgment

**Holding:** The denial of this municipal defendant's ordinary liability defense does not qualify for immediate collateral-order review under §1291 because the defense remains effectively reviewable after final judgment. The officers' distinct immunity from suit does not give the municipality a corresponding right to avoid trial.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), Part I, unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Cohen v. Beneficial Industrial Loan Corp. retains its cumulative collateral-order requirements. Digital Equipment Corp. v. Desktop Direct, Inc. is applied through its independently controlling adequate-final-review ground. Mitchell v. Forsyth is distinguished because the officials' immunity from suit is not the county's liability defense. Monell v. Department of Social Services supplies no municipal immunity from all trial.

**Independent alternative holding:** An order that expressly leaves the county's final-policymaker question open for further presentation and a later legal ruling does not conclusively determine that question under Cohen. This failure independently defeats collateral-order review; the mere denial of the pending motions does not resolve a question the court expressly reserves.

**Proposition-level authority for alternative holding:** Ginsburg's Part III, eight Justices; Stone-Zsela does not join.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Cohen is applied to the express reservation of the disputed question. Coopers & Lybrand v. Livesay is applied to the requirement of a conclusive, rather than tentative, determination. Neither decision is enlarged into a rule admitting every later definitive ruling to immediate appeal.

##### A proper immunity appeal does not authorize this separate county appeal

**Holding:** A court of appeals may not decide a separate party's nonappealable liability defense merely because a related officers' immunity appeal is properly before it and resolving the defense would save litigation. The county attribution issue here is neither inextricably intertwined with nor necessary to meaningful review of the officers' qualified-immunity questions, and no other authorized appellate route is established.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), Part II, unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Abney v. United States and United States v. Stanley are applied to limits on adding otherwise unauthorized issues to a proper immediate appeal. Cohen and Digital Equipment remain the source of the separate collateral-order inquiry. Earlier examples of connected review are not overruled or converted into a general pendent-party jurisdiction doctrine.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Part IV of Ginsburg’s opinion, joined by all eight Associate Justices, expressly leaves undecided whether genuinely inextricable or necessary connected review is available at all and what its scope would be. Stone-Zsela’s affirmative view is separate and noncontrolling. Section 1292(b) requires the District Judge's written statement that an order involves a controlling question of law, substantial ground for difference of opinion, and that an immediate appeal may materially advance the litigation's ultimate termination. Application must be made within ten days; the court of appeals may then permit the appeal in its discretion. Application alone does not stay District Court proceedings unless the District Judge, the court of appeals, or a judge thereof orders a stay. No qualifying certification or permission is established here. Section 1292(e) permits additional interlocutory-appeal rules prescribed under §2072; §2072(c) permits rules defining finality under §1291. Those are rulemaking authorizations, subject to §2072's prohibition on abridging, enlarging, or modifying substantive rights, and are not an operative rule allowing this appeal. No mandamus relief is sought or granted here; that distinct route retains its own requirements.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Commission's appellate disposition under §§1983 and 1985(3) is vacated and remanded with instructions to dismiss its interlocutory appeal. Its surviving federal claims return to their preappeal District Court posture without a liability or summary-judgment ruling. Officers' immunity dispositions and separately declined City and individual state-law appeals are undisturbed; no county state-law count remained.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Clinton v. Jones, May 27, 1997, permits connected review of the categorical trial protection and its alternative docket-control basis within the same integrated temporal-immunity order. Independent liability questions and ordinary later scheduling orders remain outside that holding.

### Component-specific vacatur and independent relief

#### New York State Conference of Blue Cross & Blue Shield Plans v. Travelers Insurance Co. / Pataki v. Travelers Insurance Co. / Hospital Association of New York State v. Travelers Insurance Co.

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 93-1408; 93-1414; 93-1415.
**Decided:** April 26, 1995.

For the other questions in this decision, see Labor and Employment — ERISA preemption and hospital-payment measures (The reviewed 13% hospital differential is not preempted; The additional commercial-insurer 11% charge is not preempted; The conditional HMO assessment is not preempted).

##### The reserved direct-plan claim remains live after limited vacatur

**Holding:** Insofar as the reviewed judgment or associated ERISA hospital-surcharge injunction adjudicates or protects a direct self-funded application left unresolved here, that portion is vacated and remanded for consideration under the announced law and any properly presented independent ground. Vacatur removes the prior adjudication’s force as a continuing final entitlement to protection without rejecting the claim, guaranteeing interim relief, disturbing independently surviving FEHBA or stop-loss relief, or imposing a payment or escrow command.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Souter), unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- United States v. X-Citement Video, Inc., November 29, 1994: applies its distinction between an available judgment-defending alternative and a required immediate decision, preserving a lawful remand without treating the alternative as a proved entitlement to relief.
- FMC Corp. v. Holliday, 498 U.S. 52: preserves its distinct insured/self-funded statutory inquiries, without deciding the reserved hospital-payor application by analogy alone.

This vacatur is expressly chosen for the reviewed unresolved component. It establishes no universal rule that a remand preserves an injunction, that a reversal automatically dissolves every order, or that reservation requires interim protection.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The independent FEHBA protection against the 13% and 11% charges in the experience-rated setting survives; community-rated coverage remains reserved and no FEHBA challenge to the 9% assessment is decided. Actuarial Letter relief on paragraphs 1–5 remains undisturbed; rejection of the challenge to paragraphs 6–7 was not appealed. Stop-loss relief is distinct from direct-plan hospital-surcharge protection. The Court finds no separate direct-self-funded injunction, mandate date, escrow distribution or stay termination; removal of an ERISA ground leaves independent protection of the same payment intact.

**Operative remedy or transition:** In all three dockets, reverse the decided commercial-insurance and HMO ERISA rulings and dissolve the associated ERISA relief; vacate reviewed direct-self-funded adjudication and ERISA surcharge protection insofar as they reach the reserved application, and remand that live claim. This removes no independently surviving FEHBA or Actuarial Letter protection and decides no direct-plan merits or right to interim relief. No new payment, escrow, reimbursement or accrued-liability command is entered.

### Defending an existing favorable judgment

#### United States v. X-Citement Video, Inc., 513 U.S. 64

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-723.
**Decided:** November 29, 1994.

For the other questions in this decision, see Criminal Procedure — Knowledge of regulated sexual depictions (The enacted knowledge term reaches minority and sexually explicit character); First Amendment — Facial challenges to child-pornography definitions (The asserted age-eighteen line does not establish substantial facial overbreadth; The complete lascivious-exhibition category defeats the word-substitution facial attack).

##### The two renewed alternatives may defend the existing favorable judgment

**Holding:** Respondents may defend the existing appellate reversal on the renewed age-eighteen and lascivious-wording grounds that appear in the record, although the court of appeals rejected those grounds, because they seek no enlargement of their rights or reduction of the Government’s rights under that judgment. The Court reaches those two alternatives; the separate actual-or-simulated bestiality and sadistic-or-masochistic-abuse objection is not established as renewed and is not decided.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Part II, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer: six Justices. Stone-Zsela does not join this reach rationale.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- *United States v. American Railway Express Co.*, 265 U.S. 425 (1924), applies the prevailing party’s ability to defend the existing judgment on a ground appearing in the record, even one rejected below, without seeking greater relief.
- *Dandridge v. Williams*, 397 U.S. 471 (1970), preserves a lawful remand option rather than imposing a duty to decide every available alternative.
- *Izumi Seimitsu Kogyo Kabushiki Kaisha v. U.S. Philips Corp.*, 510 U.S. 27 (1993), remains applicable to an attempt to overturn a distinct adverse judgment; defending this already favorable judgment does not undo its presented-question rule.
- *Caspari v. Bohlen*, 510 U.S. 383 (1994), and *Madsen v. Women’s Health Center, Inc.*, 512 U.S. 753 (1994), retain their limited subsidiary-question and exceptional-review boundaries; the Court grants no general enlargement of review based on briefing alone.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Ninth Circuit is reversed and the case remanded, 7–2. The facial-scienter invalidation and the two renewed facial-definition defenses do not sustain the appellate judgment. The existing minority-knowledge finding is recognized; the indictment-scienter, actual-tape-content and implementation issues remain. No count is unconditionally reinstated or invalidated, and no acquittal, automatic retrial or harmlessness finding is ordered.

### Federal merits failure and statutory jurisdiction

#### Young v. Northern Illinois Conference of United Methodist Church

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1917.
**Decided:** January 17, 1995.

For the other questions in this decision, see First Amendment — Ministerial selection and civil liability (Ministerial selection claims encounter a constitutional defense).

##### The defense defeats the claims on their merits, not statutory jurisdiction

**Holding:** A federal court with jurisdiction under Title VII §706(f)(3) and 28 U.S.C. §1331 over a substantial Title VII claim exercises that jurisdiction when it decides that the Religion Clauses defeat the claim; it must enter a substantive dismissal rather than characterize this defense as a withdrawal of subject-matter jurisdiction. Young's pleaded claims are substantial federal claims, and the remand is for entry of a merits dismissal of the stated ministerial advancement-and-retention claims, not a discrimination trial or an award of relief.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Souter), unanimous on both propositions.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Bell v. Hood, 327 U.S. 678 (1946): applies the distinction between a substantial federal claim's jurisdictional basis and its legal defeat; the immaterial-jurisdiction-device and wholly insubstantial-or-frivolous exceptions are not reached.
- Kokkonen v. Guardian Life Insurance Co. of America, 511 U.S. 375 (1994): preserves the need for a statutory or otherwise lawful jurisdictional basis; Title VII and §1331 supply that basis here, unlike the separate enforcement claim in Kokkonen.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The constitutional defense rests on Young's undisputed reported ministerial role and her selection-dependent claims, not a comprehensive definition of minister. No fraud or collusion is alleged; the Court neither adopts nor rejects an exception for it. The relevant church decision is that of the highest body to which this dispute was actually carried, without a new exhaustion requirement reaching the denomination's ultimate hierarchy. RFRA's invocation, preservation, and application to this suit remain undecided. A genuinely severable salary, property, tort, contract, or other claim remains outside this adjudication only if independently pleaded and resolvable without deciding doctrine, ministerial fitness, or the selection of religious leaders; no such claim is found to exist here.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The jurisdictional characterization is vacated and merits dismissal of the selection-dependent Title VII claims is required. Eight Justices vacate the jurisdictional judgment and remand for substantive dismissal; Stone-Zsela would additionally affirm the dismissal on the corrected merits basis. The controlling mandate orders no discrimination trial, reinstatement, compelled reconsideration or financial relief.

### Preserved claims and disavowed legal arguments

#### Lebron v. National Railroad Passenger Corp.

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1525.
**Decided:** February 21, 1995.

For the other questions in this decision, see First Amendment — Government-created corporations (Special federal creation and permanent majority appointment make Amtrak governmental); Federal Courts — Relief after reversal of governmental-actor status (Governmental status requires a remand, not an immediate display order).

##### The Court may consider this included legal argument despite its earlier disavowal

**Holding:** The Court elects to consider Amtrak's governmental-entity status despite Lebron's earlier express disavowal of that argument because it supplies a legal basis for the same preserved First Amendment claim, falls within the governmental-actor question presented, and can be resolved on the established institutional record without a new factual theory or additional findings. This bounded exercise of review creates no obligation to consider every abandoned argument and does not dispense with limits on distinct claims, questions, requested relief, factual stipulations, or missing material facts.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's reachability portion, joined by Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: seven Justices; Stone-Zsela does not join that rationale.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Yee v. City of Escondido, 503 U.S. 519 (1992): applies its distinction between a supporting argument and a new claim, together with its independent question-presented limit; the present decision separately addresses deliberate disavowal in this setting.
- Bray v. Alexandria Women's Health Clinic, 506 U.S. 263 (1993): distinguishes the unadjudicated separate hindrance theory, outside the granted questions and lacking a necessary finding; its scope limits remain operative.
- Bankers Life & Casualty Co. v. Crenshaw, 486 U.S. 71 (1988): applies, through Yee, the allowance of supporting arguments for a preserved federal claim, without erasing this Court's discretion over deliberately excluded grounds.
- Kamen v. Kemper Financial Services, Inc., 500 U.S. 90 (1991): preserves the distinction between legal arguments and the claim to which they relate; no unrelated claim is introduced.
- United States v. Mezzanatto, decided January 18, 1995: distinguishes the actual, limited evidentiary agreement there from this decision whether to consider an included argument; its waiver rule does not make every litigation choice an irrevocable surrender of an entire constitutional claim.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Second Circuit is reversed and remanded, 8–1, to adjudicate the unreviewed First Amendment and forum questions with Amtrak treated as governmental and reconsider associated state-law dispositions under their own rules. The display injunction is not automatically reinstated; no final display, damages, contract or TDI termination relief is awarded.

### Direct review of three-judge merits judgments

#### Johnson v. Miller (Miller v. Johnson / Abrams v. Johnson / United States v. Johnson)

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 94-631; 94-797; 94-929.
**Decided:** June 29, 1995.

**Companion or consolidated matters:** The appellate alignment is Johnson v. Miller; the three docket aliases identify one constitutional challenge and one reviewed judgment for Georgia.

For the other questions in Johnson v. Miller (Miller v. Johnson / Abrams v. Johnson / United States v. Johnson), decided June 29, 1995, Nos. 94-631; 94-797; 94-929. see Federal Courts — Standing and representation injury (Personal exposure and standing are distinct from success on the merits); Election Law — Territorial racial design and constitutional deprivation (Racial predominance alone does not establish the asserted constitutional violation; The asserted sorting, status and representation inferences do not establish a distinct deprivation here; Absence of federal compulsion does not itself prohibit voluntary inclusion; District shape is evidence, not a threshold requirement; The supported predominance finding is accepted without dictating its legal consequence).

##### The merits denial of an injunction is directly reviewable

**Holding:** A substantial constitutional challenge to congressional apportionment, adjudicated by the required three-judge district court through a merits denial of injunctive relief, falls within 28 U.S.C. §§2284 and 1253. This appeal meets those requirements; a nonmerits dismissal would require its own review-channel analysis.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone, Part I-B, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes.

**Limits and questions not reached:** A serious losing claim is not necessarily wholly insubstantial. The panel's merits injunction denial establishes this review channel; no unlocated mailing return is treated as a procedural defect and no universal waiver is presumed.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Goosby v. Osser: applies the narrow wholly-insubstantial exception; a serious losing constitutional claim is not necessarily jurisdictionally frivolous.
- Gonzalez v. Automatic Employees Credit Union and MTM, Inc. v. Baxley: distinguish nonmerits dispositions from this merits denial of injunctive relief.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm the three-judge district court's judgment for Georgia and denial of injunctive relief in Nos. 94-631, 94-797 and 94-929, without remand. No injunction or redrawing order is unwound and no new injury inquiry, map order, election cancellation or retained supervision is imposed. The judgment does not certify the entire plan or adjudicate separate claims against federal officials.

#### Bush v. Vera; Lawson v. Vera; United States v. Vera

**Docket or dockets:** 94-805, 94-806 and 94-988
**Decided:** June 13, 1996

##### Do the five district residents have personal-assignment standing, and can Chen share that basis?

**Holding:** Under Miller, voters personally subjected to challenged electoral assignment may litigate the alleged unequal treatment when their own exposure, causation and likely redress are established, even if their substantive theory fails. Mere residence or disagreement with racial districting does not suffice; these residents' own assignments provide the required relationship without additional financial loss, electoral defeat or denied service. Chen's lack of personal connection to Districts 18, 29 and 30 supplies no independent standing, but does not defeat their affected residents' claims.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), all eight Associate Justices. Standing disposition unanimous; Stone-Zsela agrees in the judgment only.

**Material application:** Blum and Powers challenge their own District 18 assignments, Thomas and Vera District 29, and Orcutt District 30. Chen resides in District 25 and alleges no other relevant connection. Each challenged district has an affected plaintiff; no broader nonresident theory is decided.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Lujan's stage-appropriate factual support remains required; unsupported allegations do not invariably suffice after trial. Configuration, confusion, ballot and campaign evidence is not converted into a separate injury. Standing establishes neither a merits violation nor an injunction entitlement.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Miller's personal-assignment rule is retained. Northeastern Florida separates personal exposure from winning an ultimate benefit; Warth separates standing from success. Lujan preserves personal injury, causation, redress and stage-appropriate proof.

##### Are the three injunction appeals directly reviewable?

**Holding:** A required three-judge court's merits grant of injunctive relief in this substantial constitutional apportionment controversy supports direct review under 28 U.S.C. §§2284 and 1253. Governing precedent's strong merits answer does not make the constitutional controversy jurisdictionally frivolous.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's threshold opinion, all eight Associates; Stone-Zsela agrees only in the threshold judgment.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Miller distinguishes merits injunction judgments from nonmerits dismissals requiring their own review-channel analysis. The merits and dependent-relief holdings for these same dockets appear under Election Law, Territorial racial design and constitutional deprivation.

### Removal and interlocutory review

#### Things Remembered, Inc. v. Petrarca, 516 U.S. 124

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1530.
**Decided:** December 5, 1995.

##### Review of a covered remand when removal also invokes bankruptcy jurisdiction

**Holding:** When a removed case is remanded to the State court from which it came because of a timely raised removal-procedure defect or lack of subject-matter jurisdiction, §1447(d), read with §1447(c), bars appellate review even if removal also invoked §1452. The then-applicable exception allows review on appeal or otherwise of an order remanding a case removed under §1443; that exception is not implicated here.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous. Ginsburg and Stevens's additional §1452(b) equitable-remand ground has two votes and is not a separate controlling alternative.

**Material application:** The actual stated remand ground was untimely removal, raised by a timely motion. The thirty-day limit applies to a procedural-remand motion; lack of subject-matter jurisdiction is a distinct covered ground.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court does not make all bankruptcy remands unreviewable, determine the ultimate meaning of equitable grounds under §1452(b), decide the correctness of the covered remand, or decide reviewability of Cohill remands outside these grounds.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm dismissal for lack of appellate jurisdiction; the state-court remand remains undisturbed. No lease or bankruptcy merits are adjudicated.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Thermtron confines §1447(d) to §1447(c)'s covered grounds; Rice applies the ordinary bar across removal statutes absent contrary direction; Gravitt bars review even for asserted error in a covered ground. Germain requires coexistence of overlapping statutes without positive conflict: §1452(b)'s additional work does not repeal §1447(d). Cohill's separate reviewability question remains open.

#### Yamaha Motor Corp., U.S.A. v. Calhoun, 516 U.S. 199

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1387.
**Decided:** January 9, 1996.

##### Issues included in a permitted §1292(b) appeal

**Holding:** A permitted interlocutory appeal under §1292(b) reaches issues fairly included in the certified order, not only the particular question formulated for certification. The displacement question is included here because it underlies the appealed partial-summary-judgment order; jurisdiction reaches no unrelated order in the action.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's Part I; all eight Associates join. Stone-Zsela agrees only in this component's judgment.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Section 1292(b) requires written district-court certification of a controlling legal question, substantial ground for difference of opinion, and material advancement of the litigation's termination; an application within ten days permits discretionary appellate acceptance. The application alone creates no stay. Those predicates were satisfied here and are not displaced.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Sustain review of the displacement issue within the certified order. The substantive death-remedy holding appears under Maritime Law, Yamaha, January 9, 1996, No. 94-1387.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Swint's prohibition on adding a separate nonappealable county defense to an officer's collateral-order appeal remains. Its distinct §1292(b) route was actually followed here; collateral-order jurisdiction is not enlarged.

### Reasoned grants, vacaturs, and remands

#### Lawrence v. Chater, 516 U.S. 163

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-9323.
**Decided:** January 8, 1996.

##### Procedural justification for reconsideration after a material agency development

**Holding:** Under §2106, an intervening or sufficiently recent development makes grant, vacatur, and remand potentially appropriate when there is a reasonable probability that the judgment rests on a premise the lower court would reject upon reconsideration and that reconsideration may affect the outcome. The Court must independently weigh equities, including finality, lower-court independence, delay, cost, and manipulative litigation; a Government request alone does not justify vacatur.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion; Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer directly adopt the rule and application. Stone-Zsela's judgment-only reasonable-possibility formulation is not controlling. The public instrument discloses the writings and joins, not a petition tally.

**Material application:** The agency's new acknowledgment that it examines the constitutional validity of state intestacy and paternity requirements at least in some circumstances sufficiently bears on the unexamined state-law premise of this benefits claim to warrant reconsideration.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No present legal error, parentage, benefits entitlement, constitutional invalidity, mandatory agency review, or deferential weight is established. The receiving court determines the position's relevance, lawful authority and effect and may reach the same outcome. The North Carolina amendment's claimant-specific predicates are unproved and supply no independent vacatur ground.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate and return the Fourth Circuit judgment for consideration of the identified agency development. Lawfully available threshold requirements and defenses remain; forfeited defenses are not revived and independently closed issues are not reopened.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Henry separates reconsideration from a merits decision; Alvarado supports remand practice without surrendering judicial responsibility. Thomas Jefferson University supplies no automatic statutory or constitutional deference; MCI preserves independent statutory boundaries. McCarthy and Darby's distinct exhaustion rules remain unchanged.

#### Stutson v. United States, 516 U.S. 193

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-8988.
**Decided:** January 8, 1996.

##### Reconsideration of an unresolved criminal-appeal excusable-neglect premise

**Holding:** Section 2106 permits limited reconsideration when the combination of a potentially important governing formulation, the Government's changed legal position, a one-working-day misdirected filing, and an unexplained affirmance leaves a substantial question whether the correct excusable-neglect inquiry was performed. These circumstances justify reconsideration of Rule 4(b) in light of Pioneer; no one circumstance, including a Government concession, makes vacatur automatic.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer directly adopt this case-specific ground and remedy. No petition vote tally is announced.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Pioneer was already available; an unexplained affirmance is not presumed erroneous or to have ignored it. The Court does not equate every Rule 4(b) application with bankruptcy enlargement, transplant Rule 9006(b)(2)–(3)'s separate restrictions, find excusable neglect, or accept the late notice. Pioneer's circumstances include prejudice, delay and its effects, reason and reasonable controllability, and good faith; no factor is invariably decisive and counsel attribution remains.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate and remand. The Eleventh Circuit determines Pioneer's proper effect under Rule 4(b) and arranges necessary district-court findings. A renewed lawful denial remains possible; neither a merits appeal nor conviction or sentence relief is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Pioneer supplies the potentially relevant formulation at its Chapter 11 scope, retaining all-circumstances review, counsel attribution and rule-specific limits. Henry separates reconsideration from final merits relief; Alvarado preserves independent judicial justification. No full Rule 4(b) merits extension is decided.

#### Department of State v. Legal Assistance for Vietnamese Asylum Seekers, Inc.

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-1521.

**Decided:** October 21, 1996.

##### Reconsideration after the intervening visa-processing enactment

**Holding:** Section 633 directly addresses the processing-location premise of the judgment below and creates a reasonable probability that reconsideration will alter an outcome-material statutory premise or continuing relief. Independent consideration of the equities warrants vacatur and a confined remand, without deciding the old-law merits or the new provision's final construction and temporal reach.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's opinion of the Court, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court leaves the old-law merits, the new clause's construction and temporal operation, surviving APA and constitutional claims, and lawful relief undecided. The enactment does not by itself moot those claims or establish a right to asylum, a visa, Hong Kong processing, damages or continuing foreign protection.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lawrence is applied to an actual intervening statute without enlarging its agency-development holding. Landgraf and Rivers remain controlling on their distinct temporal-reach questions; the remand decides no conflict between their previously published rationales.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The D.C. Circuit judgment is vacated for prompt, bounded consideration of section 633 and surviving claims and relief. The same lawful result remains possible. No forfeited defense or independently closed issue is reopened; additional factfinding is required only on identified material matters the existing record cannot resolve.

#### Lords Landing Village Condominium Council of Unit Owners v. Continental Insurance Co.

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-1033.
**Decided:** June 2, 1997.

##### An actually entertained untimely rehearing request postpones review finality

**Holding:** An untimely rehearing request postpones finality for Supreme Court review only when the lower court actually entertains it on the merits; mere filing or an unentertained request for leave does not suffice. The requested response, competing Sheets positions and express considered denial establish actual entertainment here, making the December 27 petition timely from December 16 for review of the original judgment.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court. Individual Court-opinion joins and the petition tally are not publicly announced; the published separate writings disclose their own positions only.

**Material application:** The lower court requested and received adversarial briefing and expressly considered the renewed request on its merits. Its simultaneous denial of leave does not negate that actual consideration.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Under 1995 Rule 13, the effective rehearing disposition rather than the mandate supplies the date. Section 2106 cannot cure untimeliness or create an extension; neither every second rehearing request nor independent recall review renews original-judgment review.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Bowman and Idaho Power distinguish substantive entertainment from filing alone, unentertained leave requests and formal repetition.

##### Actual prior consideration does not categorically bar a justified state-law GVR

**Holding:** Under Lawrence, sufficiently recent authoritative state law can justify grant, vacatur and remand when there is a reasonable probability that reconsideration will reject an outcome-material premise and may affect the result, and independent equities favor a return. Sheets meets that standard here despite predating the judgment and receiving later consideration: its rejection of the articulated accident definition bears directly on the coverage rationale, while the confined remand leaves every actual indemnity issue to the lower court.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court; no individual Court-opinion joins or petition tally are publicly announced.

**Material application:** Sheets directly rejects the accident definition underlying the reviewed reasoning, creating a reasonable probability of an outcome-material change despite its earlier date and later adversarial consideration. Finality, lower-court independence, expense and delay count separately; no tactical withholding or unaffected sufficient ground is established.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Sheets's defense-potentiality setting does not establish indemnity after verdict. Statutory misrepresentation without required scienter does not prove expected property damage; the other verdicts do not prove its absence. Economic-loss dismissal does not convert underlying negligence into intentional conduct. The Court creates no coverage rule, error presumption from unexplained denial, or requirement for a longer opinion.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Grant review, vacate the August 6 judgment and remand for reconsideration under Sheets. Preserved independent grounds, causation, property-damage categories, exclusions and indemnity remain for the lower court, which may reach the same result. No separate invalidation of the December order, mandatory hearing, revived forfeited claim or damages award follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lawrence's reasonable-probability and independent-equities requirements are applied; mere possible relevance does not suffice. Sheets supplies the state accident definition without a federal indemnity holding.

### Filing controls

#### Attwood v. Singletary, 516 U.S. 297

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-6710.

**Decided:** January 22, 1996.

##### This particular application supports financial leave

**Holding:** Supported inability to prepay and an arguable present petition warrant financial leave on this record; prior volume and state abuse do not themselves establish the particular frivolousness or malice needed for the proposed Rule 39.8 denial. Granting leave does not establish the truth of the allegations, entitlement to certiorari, or future financial eligibility.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer; eight Justices. Stone-Zsela concurs in the result on separate grounds.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No precise asset balance is found. Financial leave does not establish the truth of the access allegations, constitutional invalidity of the state counsel-signature condition, certiorari entitlement or future eligibility. The certiorari denial supplies no merits holding and no disclosed petition poll.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Financial leave granted for this petition; no payment interval or future fee-and-form condition imposed.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Martin and Anderson are applied to the particular submission; Zatko's power to deny an actually frivolous or malicious filing remains.

##### The proposed future condition lacks its separate supporting showing

**Holding:** A prospective fee-and-form condition on a category of future filings requires support tied to the applicant, that category, and the inadequacy of treating individual submissions. Extensive state litigation alone does not establish that showing for the proposed condition on future noncriminal certiorari petitions in this Court.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer; eight Justices. Stone-Zsela concurs in the result on separate grounds.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No universal warning prerequisite, numerical threshold or obligation to exhaust every conceivable lesser measure is imposed. Paid and criminal alternatives narrow a proposed restriction but do not prove necessity or fit. Neutral filing rules and sanctions for proved misconduct remain available.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Martin, Day, Sassower, and Anderson supply the distinct prospective showing; McDonald and Sindram retain their bounded process-protection authority.

#### Jones v. ABC-TV, 516 U.S. 363

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-7186.

**Decided:** February 26, 1996.

##### Whether an incomplete financial showing establishes ineligibility or default

**Holding:** Rule 39.1 requires the prescribed motion and notarized affidavit or §1746 declaration unless an applicable appointed-counsel exception exists. Incomplete references to financial circumstances do not establish eligibility, ability to pay, failure to submit required papers or falsity; a terminal financial determination requires its own supported basis.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), joined by all seven other participating Justices; Breyer does not participate.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No appointed-counsel exception or required financial particulars are established. The ruling imposes no refiling duty, deadline or default consequence; no financial-leave or certiorari outcome is decided.

##### Whether defective underlying claims establish the Rule 39.8 predicate for the present petition

**Holding:** Rule 39.8 requires assessment of the particular paper; ordinary pleading failure is not automatically frivolousness. The defects in Jones's media, compulsory-publication and derivative §1986 theories do not resolve his distinct objections to actual paid pro se filing restrictions and denial of requested appellate briefing, so the present showing does not support the proposed denial.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), joined by all seven other participating Justices; Breyer does not participate.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The allegations' truth, unlawfulness of lower restrictions, recusal and appellate procedure, indigence and entitlement to review are not established. Supported denial remains available and is permitted, not compelled, by the Rule; no petition poll is announced.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Martin, Anderson and Attwood's current-paper inquiry applies; Zatko retains supported denial for frivolousness or malice.

##### Whether lower-court abuse alone supports future noncriminal-certiorari restrictions

**Holding:** A prospective condition requires support tied to the applicant, the relevant filing category and the inadequacy of individual treatment. Lower-court abuse findings alone do not establish those predicates for Jones's future noncriminal certiorari submissions, and preserving paid and criminal routes does not supply the missing justification.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; seven Justices. Stone-Zsela concurs in the result on a broader separate ground; Breyer does not participate.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No categorical invalidation of prospective controls, universal warning prerequisite, numerical threshold or exhaustion of every conceivable lesser measure is required. The broader concurrence does not control.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Martin, Day, Sassower, Anderson and Attwood's independent prospective showing applies; McDonald and Sindram retain targeted process-protection authority.

#### Shieh v. Kakita; Shieh v. United States Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit; Shieh v. Krieger, 517 U.S. 343

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 95-7587, 95-7588 and 95-7589.
**Decided:** April 1, 1996.
**Companion or consolidated matters:** Three separately assessed individual financial motions and petitions, together with a separate proposed prospective filing condition.

##### The three separate declarations support individual financial leave

**Holding:** Each of Shieh's separately signed declarations establishes financial eligibility on this record. The grants rest on the individual applications' actual means and create neither an irrebuttable-affidavit rule nor derivative relief for the corporation in two captions.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Part I, unanimous.

**Material application:** Current sworn answers reported no employment, recent income, cash, accounts or listed valuable property; former earnings were not present resources. A recent appended account of very large gross income created a genuine tension but identified no dated receipts or accessible assets in the reporting periods. The later specific declarations sufficed without authenticating or declaring the appended account false; a blank dependents line claimed no dependent expense, and a California fee grant did not control federal eligibility.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Rule 39.1 requires the prescribed motion identifying other-court leave requests and dispositions and a Form 4 notarized affidavit or §1746 declaration. Its federal statutory counsel-appointment exception requires citation of the appointment statute and was not invoked. Particular clarification remains permissible. Ordinary form, service, timeliness, jurisdiction and substance rules survive; no payment, booklet conversion, deadline or underlying relief is imposed.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Attwood supports an adequate financial showing despite imperfect particulars; Jones involved unestablished financial particulars, unlike these declarations. Rowland requires an entity's own authorized actual-means showing and lawful representation; personal leave creates no corporate entitlement.

##### Particular notice and reopening content defeats the proposed current-paper denials

**Holding:** The present showing does not support Rule 39.8 denial of any of these three petitions because each identifies a concrete notice or reopening contention not conclusively disposed of by its other defects. The result depends on those particular grounds, not an immunity for broad allegations or the mere use of notice language.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Parts II-A, II-B and II-C, unanimous, separately addressing each petition.

**Material application:** Kakita identifies allegedly unserved summary-judgment papers and connects nonservice to reopening; the appellate recital of record review and closing of those dockets does not answer that contention. The Ninth Circuit petition documents a request for the show-cause paper and raises its own notice and fourteen-day-response objection; the restriction's retained counseled and certified-nonfrivolous routes do not establish adequate notice. Krieger separately connects its allegedly unserved motion and order to Rule 60(b)(6)/Molloy reopening; appellate review and the no-new-arguments rationale do not conclusively answer that ground. Missing papers establish neither service failure nor a conclusive defense, and no independent malice finding supports denial.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The petitions' exaggerated no-review and complete-ban claims do not enlarge reopening review. Fax evidence proves the request, not failed mailing. The Court adopts no actual-receipt requirement, general fourteen-day rule, treaty or foreign-proceeding entitlement, unlawful-restriction finding or substantive reopening construction. Rule 39.8 permits rather than requires a denial upon the necessary particular-paper showing. Financial leave and screening decide neither certiorari nor the underlying merits.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Zatko preserves supported particular-paper denials. Martin, Anderson, Attwood and Jones require separate assessment of concrete present content. Denton bars resolution of genuine factual disputes through ordinary disbelief but supplies no complete Supreme Court Rule 39.8 test.

##### Prospective noncriminal-certiorari restrictions require a separate applicant-and-category showing

**Holding:** A future noncriminal-certiorari condition requires support tied to the applicant, the relevant filing category and the inadequacy of treating particular submissions. This proposed fee-and-form condition lacks that showing and is declined.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Part III, unanimous. Stone-Zsela's additional stronger presumption is noncontrolling.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Lower restrictions and repeated litigation alone do not establish abuse of this Court's category or inadequate individual treatment. No sequence of this Court's earlier Shieh denials, warnings or failed limited responses supplies the missing connection on this record; no universal warning prerequisite, numerical threshold or requirement that every conceivable lesser measure fail follows. Retained paid and criminal routes narrow but do not justify the proposal. Existing lawful restrictions, neutral filing rules, finality, jurisdiction and sanctions for proved misconduct remain.

**Operative remedy or transition:** All three individual financial motions are granted and each current-paper and future-condition proposal declined, unanimously. The petitions remain for separate ordinary consideration; no certiorari outcome or new restriction follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Martin, Day, Sassower, Anderson, Attwood and Jones preserve the separate prospective inquiry. McDonald and Sindram retain bounded targeted-control authority when its predicates are established.

### Ancillary jurisdiction and judgment enforcement

#### Peacock v. Thomas, 516 U.S. 349

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1453.

**Decided:** February 21, 1996.

##### Whether the later collection action has original ERISA jurisdiction

**Holding:** A later action imposing the corporate judgment on Peacock alleges no underlying ERISA or plan violation supporting the asserted federal jurisdiction. Veil piercing is a means of imposing liability, not an independent statutory violation; §502(a)(3) supplies relief for the obligations it identifies, not equitable relief at large.
